                      1. Tan zhoumo de dasudn

            4§33*% Self-Explanatory Characters

                 TAC PBA BUI FA, A LES SD AS ie A AR I
            HK, ArH Ss ern. PON:

                  When people in ancient times were creating Chinese characters, they used
            abstract signs to signify the abstract things which could not be expressed by specific
            images. For example:

            iM Character        EIR Picture  MFA Meaning                               |

                     =          --                                                                  |

                                             x F "--" | The Numeral "one". |

                                                                                                |
                                             a F "=" | The Numeral "two". |

                                                                                                |

            a i Ji                           HF "=" | The Numeral "three".

                                =            fe u-F A it, Above the horizon.
                                "--          fe #u-F RF i, Below the horizon.

            >                                fe "K" FFigw--kh, SSR,

                                             A dot is added at the lower part of "A"
                                             to indicate the root of a tree.

                                                                                                      |

                                             fi. "Th F kitjo--s., eS SRA.

                                             A dot is added at the upper part of "*"
                                             to indicate the tip ofa tree.

  Common He who has never been to the Great Wall is not a true man
                     "AS BI AEE", aEBR "SB" AEE "ANE"| "RA" Ae

                 "ABUT FEY A" "SRE" 6 KATY BE EIA ELAR A AEA EE, HET
                FAS LEM -- Fh A AS A, EEE, BESS AIA SEAR, AR Ta, ARE

                                                               "TEI" Oo

                         In the saying "AFI AERFIL", "SE" means "not", and "#f7L" means "awesome
                     person" or "hero". The saying means that a person who has never been to the Great Wall
                     is not a hero. As a metaphor for positive spirit, it defines a hero as one who tries hard and
                     works out the problems he faces.

                   In cetnee, « `V + /4" indicates the direction of an action, with "#" indicating
           the direction towards the speaker and "3" the direction away from the speaker. The most

           frequently used verbs include _ oR, dee Hn, [a], "aot" and "i" as well as some
           verbs wev'e learned, such as "35". ,"ar" and"#". For example:

                 (1) amit. ke, (EAC, (HEHAARE)

7 HK: Location Word+V#+Numeral+Measure Word+ N

The Expression of Existence: Location Word + V4 + Numeral + Measure Word + N

HOE D"HUe E""Loacation"WEoEr"d+N V4+NP" P RatAE HoA n ", 7AG, BE"P "VUiLt

DK", ARE "RRA" "ARH" SE A lH ak eidR A. NM:

       In Chinese, the structure "Location Word + V# + NP" means there is something
somewhere. In this structure, the verb "ji", "5", "AA", or "fE" etc. is often used, and
the noun phrase is usually an indefinite object, for example "--7S+4" (a book) and "JL
A, (several people)" rather than "iX7S-+ (this book)" and "J#] AFH (Manager Zhou)." For
example:

| Drei 5 Texts in Pinyin

                    1. Zdi jidoshi
                         XidGoming: Zhé shi nimen bisdi de zhaopian ma?
                           Maké: Shi, zhé shi women bisdi hou zhao de.
                       XiGoming: Zhdo de bucud, nimen dou shi yi ge nidnji de ma?
                           Maké: Bu shi. Nage you gao you pidoliang de ntihdir shi ér nidnjf de.
                       Xidoming: Pangbian nage ndzhe shi xido de rén shi shéi?
                           Make: Na shi wo!

                      2. Zai jidoshi
                            Xidoli: Ni juede Xidohéng zénmeyang?

                        tongxué: Ta you cOngming you réqing, yé hén nuli.
                            Xidoli: Wo kan ta z6ngshi xidozhe hufdd Idoshi de wenti.

                        tdngxué: TG dul méi ge rén déu xido, yé changchang dui wé xido.
                            Xidoli: Ni shi bu shi xihuan ta a?

                        tdngxué: Xihuan ta de rén tdi dud le, ni kan naxié ndzhe xianhud zhan zai ménkdu de, dou
                                    shi déng ta de.

                    3. Zai chdoshi ménkdu
                       Xidogang: W6 youdidnr é le, women jin chdoshi mdi didnr déngxi ba.
                            Xidoli: Ho a, zhé jia chdoshi de dangGo you pidnyi you hdochi, yi kudir zhi yao ér didn jit
                                        jit yuan.
                       Xidogang: Women mai lidng kudir, hui jia chizhe dangdo kan dianshi, zenmeyang?
                            Xidoli: Hdo a, wd zai qu mdi yixié hé de.
                       XiGogang: Hézhe kaféi chi dangao, tdi hao le!

                    4, Zai fangudnr
                            jingli: Nin hao! Nin zhao shéi?
                            kérén: Nimen zhér shi bu shi yéu yi ge you nidnging you pidoliang de fuwtyudn?
                            jingli: Women zhér nidnging, pidoliang de fuwuyudn you hén dud.

         mise" Zia, Wiese. pila:

                In Chinese, "C Adj, + Adj," is used to describe two qualities existing in the same
         person or thing, such as "XC j=;278252" (both tall and pretty). For example:

         (1) XA HRRKRH,
         (2) FPURLBAALA,

         (3) RFR RFRLEE,
         (4) WLP LUVALA.

         BIER CERB: V,%&(O,)+V,(O,) The Accompanying Action: V,# (O,)+ V(O,)

              DUA PAL "V4 (0,) +V, (0,) " Aa one IB N EAT , V deatnt
         BA, FEVAEETRTTS SK. Hea "aa IZOL" Zea "SAT EI,
         JL", Fistat "wha" 5 ARak "AR". PIU:

                In Chinese, "V,#7 (O,) + V, (O,) " indicates two actions taking place at the same
         time, V, being the accompanying state or manner of V,. For example, "ftH{l 444 WX JL"

         means "they are talking" while "standing" rather than "sitting". For example:

              (1) FC EREA HL,

               (2) MALFORKRKLA A BAL,

              (3) RFAFASHAADNT,
              (4) WERRAREARIE,

        (j= A+tYRBY Wishi bu xido bai bi

    Common The one who retreated 50 steps laughs at the one who retreated 100

     SayiNG AECL" RANI ERE, PATER, FTL

    WEP, --SAGE RTA, BT AY, TARR aA

    APA, DitHe). FESABPwkA, FRAC AA], TE RAIL E ABLE RR

    HAAR, AAR          ARLA.

    "ti+2424%" originates from a story of ancient China. There were two soldiers

    who fled from a lost battle. One had run 50 steps backward, and the other 100 steps. The
    one who had retreated 50 steps laughed at the one who had retreated 100 steps, saying he
    was a coward. The truth is both of them were running away, the only difference lying in
    how far they'd run. "fi+2+%26 2" is now used metaphorically to criticize people who
    have the same problems as those they laugh at, only to a lesser degree.

1. Zdi Xidoli jid      =

péngyou: W6 tingshud ni shénti bu shifu, zénme le?

Xidoli: Qian ji tian youdidnr fa shdo, xianzdi hdodud le.

péngyou: Hé béi cha ba, zhé shi w6 wéi ni mdi de Ilicha, hén biicud.

Xidoli: Xiéxie, wd yao chi yao, bu hé cha le.

péngyou: Na hé béi shui ba.

Xidoli: Hdo de.

2. Zai dé dianhua
     Zhou taitai: Duibuqi, w6 mingtian bu néng hé nimen chiqu wanr le.

  Zhang tditai: Wei shénme? Zénme le?
     Zhou tditai: W6 érzi shéng bing le, w6 yao zai jiad zhdogt ta.

  Zhang taitai: Ta chi yao le ma? Yao bu ydo qu yiyuan?
     Zhou tditai: Buyong qu yiyuan, zudtian chile ganmdo yao, xianzai hao yixié le,

   Zhang tditai: Na women xia ci zai yigi chUgu wanr ba.

3. Zai Xidogang jia
           Xidoli: Ni zui xihuan nage jijié?

                      Used at the end of a declarative sentence, the modal particle " ['" indicates a change in
              the situation or the occurrence of a new situation. For example, "F¥#REYZE[K" means "I
              used to have money, but now I don't", implying a change. For example:

                     (1) ES AMR4A, RARALMBAAT,

                     (2) RAPLAA RILAM, RAST,

                    (3) RMASKART.

Characters  4: Associative Compounds

                 TCR ELAR Et FPS ic BS _ th fs S 2 --ISE, L
            FNRA MEEM SE E . Bld:

                  When creating Chinese characters, people in ancient times sometimes
            combined two or more single-component characters into one character. The meaning
            of the compound one was the meanings of the single-component characters added
            together. For example:

            iM} Character     EIR Picture                                      MFWSB Meaning
                     AF          ©
                                                                        KiaFeF HA--A, AAA,

                                                                        The sun and the moon are combined
                                                                        togeter to mean "bright."

                    A      .                                            A  EAE,

                                                                  »     A person is leaning against a tree.

                    ne                                            » 1"  --*ARAA---DA,

                                                                        A person is walking behind another.

                                                                        FueL, AIF,

                    A         Li». ¥                                    Hold one's hand over one's eye to

                                                                        look into the distance.

    {Qj ABR Yaoddo bingchu

    Common _ The minute the medicine is used, the disease is cured

    Saying

--=--_           "Za SRR" WEEE TA, WS bee

                 T, BABAR, HW te sere. BIE

                 418, PERLA Bi A FG I [a] A SE, Se efe ad

                       "ZE lle'? means a disease is cured as soon as
                 some medicine is used. It is used to describe a skilful
                 doctor who can prescribe just the right medicine.
                 Now it is also a metaphor of one's ability to find the crux of a problem and thus solve it
                 immediately.

                     PHAHARBIEGH Traditional Sports in China

      In China, apart from modern sports like jogging, working out in gyms, and playing balls,
some traditional sports handed down from old times are also practiced as part of people's daily
exercise, such as flying kites, kicking shuttlecocks, playing diabolos, and practising taijiquan.

Notes  Complements of Possibility Introduced by "V#4/#"

              ispA "ViG + Complements" BK "VAN + Complements" #7feNvT
       Kc PE pp BE RE a A BI AH ALA. "V7 + Complements" JR EIBsL, "VAN +

       Complements" 727EIES. "4" JG AAMAS ER AMA eA. 2
       Ainakshin, EMI sts: "Vi + Complements VA + Complements" BK "V

       4 + Complements 15" .
              In Chinese, "V4 + Complements" or "V4\ + Complements" can be used to indicate

       whether or not a result can be obtained or a goal be reached. "V/ + Complements" is the
       positive form, and "V/4\ + Complements" is the negative form. The complements used
       in this structure are usually complements of result or direction, and certain adjectives or
       verbs. The interrogative form is "V7 + Complements V4\ + Complements" or "V7} +
       Complements !4". For example:

             (1) RAFT HMR RF
            (2) RAMS, REA,

              (3) BYP G9 AKTAS LAI R IL

            (4) RRA AE

       example, "#452? " means "where is mom?", and "/|\¥iJJ~?_ " means "where is the dog?".

    Ay ABEA)FAT

           The adverb "|" and the noun "| 7-" both indicate that the action has happened not
    long ago. However, "`}]7~"' usually means several minutes ago, while "Bl" means a short
    time from the speaker's point of view, which can be several minutes, a few days, even
    months. For example, "F&I 3 "7 HE] 7+ A" (I've been in China for only two months) shows
    that the speaker considers two months a short period of time. "fl!|" is only used right before
    the verb, while "|" can be used before the verb or at the beginning of the sentence.

              A period of time refers to the length of time that an action or a state lasts, usually

       indicated by a complement of duration, such as "--%l|#}" (a quarter), "*7+/|AY" (half an
       hour), "PY" (two days), or "=~+A{" (three months) etc.

            "S+V+ + Duration+ O" ZANaVEFSEAIIN TH). fA:

               "S$ +V+ JS + Duration + O" indicates the duration of an action. For example:

             (1) WL f =F,
             (2) RANE T --P PH ARAF,
             (3) REA Z| T --Hl a?
             (4) &41 T AD AR,

        "Aa ITNT BS", RANA aE -- TY, JF AUER EAH.

                                        "S$ +V+ Ss + Duration + O + J" indicates the duration of an action which is still
                             going on. For example, "#4 S--+/)\At-4[3" means one has already read for an hour and is
                                still reading. For example:

                          (S)KRFIT---FRET.

Common One wrong move makes all moves wrong

gets       "ESR EGR" ISERECE, PRM, WRA--A ERT. BAUM
      BE AE AB ER AY . RA) AE CMT ART te i TR RE, ARAVA
      BBE DOR AE ARE FY) 0 AT URSA], CSE TIN 1 E25 A, GE
      HOM, FCaABEYT AR

           "_ 36 e gta ab" means that in a chess

      game one wrong move will make all the moves
      after it wrong. In the metaphorical sense it
      indicates if we' ve made a wrong decision, then
      every decision following it would be wrong.
      This saying tells us to be prudent when doing
      something so as to avoid mistakes.

       2 oh feattaol.

              The adverbs "\<" and "" can both be put before a verb to indicate the recurrence of
       an action or a situation. "< + V" usually means the recurrence has already happened, while
       `"f. + V" means the recurrence has not happened yet.

       "KX" AYOFP: Examples of "XQ" :

       (1) EABHRE LT ---RAT. BPRAET--A.

       Fé /o)¢¢ia)38FA1 Flexible Use of Interrogative Pronouns |

       DUA, Fa "RL" EREPIAST SED)A Cia), ZEA NTA A Sak

       FAX. BERM PhEPA K, BU PrRe T SAe A]. SHI                   oP a) HY

       TRAIN, Ja -- "Sa PEG TA, WR So EGR AS IA], 8

       PS ESI "ot" AH. PG:

       In Chinese, "`#" is used to connect two same interrogative pronouns, indicating an

       uncertain person, thing or manner. The interrogative pronoun appears twice in the sentence,

       referring to the same thing. When the two clauses of a sentence share one subject, the subject

       of the second clause can be left out; when the two clauses have different subjects, the second

       subject is put right before "i". For example:

        VS  Mes Character Note

Characters  ié73F1 Pictophonetic Characters 1

                 PURDUE, 70% AWN FA IE eS FBR I Ss A oS Ph

            Mm. BBS hBe, SSR                   eNRe.

            Around 70% of Chinese characters are pictophonetic ones, which are composed of two

            components, one indicating meaning and the other sound.

                 PARAM Sela, Ae "AAS" , BBS eA,
            Sta, (ili:

                   The pictophonetic characters of a left-right structure fall into two types. One has the
            semantic component on the left and the phonetic component on the right. For example:

             ee                 RE             ay                        WH

            Character          _ Pronounciation Phonetic Component Semantic Component

               #                ma             % (ma)                                  4

               fE               zhu            & (zhi)                                 {

                 Ahive "ABA" , BBA, EA, pan:

                   The other has the semantic component on the right and the phonetic component on the
            left. For example:

                vee                BRE                ae                    ize

            __ Character       Pronounciation  Phonetic Component    Semantic Component

                  BX                fang          % (fang)                      &

                  As                 lin           4 (ling)

                others, there must be one whom I can learn from'. In

                the metaphorical sense, it means everybody may have

                some merits that we lack, so we should learn from

Notes    Comparative Sentences 2: AEEB + Adj + --SJL/--#/2#/2 7

                "-- JL" "-- 2b" "452" "26 7" SeiBTLAA "Ee" Se a Te AF i] BY
         ja, aN Zale BE, "IL" A "8" Re22aHlFEAS
         K, "4B" BCT" Rae ARERR AK. Bilan:

                  "7G JL", "2b", "48." or "4 f" etc. can follow an adjective in a comparative
          sentence, indicating the degree of difference between things, among which ""-- JL" and "--#&"
          indicate a slight difference, while "42" and "2 J'" indicate a big difference. For example:

                (1) KHHKER--AIL,
               (2) RA KAFFE RE B=,
               (3) AKA AL EE KF FF 54

                                                                                                                                                                      °

               (4) RFA REST.

            AA "ABCA (4iLA/IBA) + Adj", Gilt:

                  The negative form is "A2ZAB ( + i A/ASA) + Adj". For example:

               (5) who ak i Ay RAG
              (6) HORA BEA
               (7) EPRYREYAPAP A EN S,
                (8) FREUD (FT) RHFRIPUOPALRH.

                             REALAN FIA 1 Expression of Approximate Numbers 1

                               DUA, AASB PTR AAT AeA. ON "RS
                           =. AK, tA, WL" &. Bilan:

                                        In Chinese, two adjacent numerals can be used together to indicate an approximate
                             number, for example, "---- ( P4) (one or two)", "P= (two or three)", "=P (three or
                                four)", "FL7` (five or six)", "E/\ (seven or eight)", and "/\JL (eight or nine)". For example:

                              (1) RARKRFI---AM)HRE,
                            (2) (Raph l SO ews, , MART!
                            (3) A AR Sl Dd 3) Be ACN a HL HE I
                              (4) FREWMUGSRIPUAR, MLALOS38,

    Common.     The two cannot be mentioned in the same breath

    DSAaIyiNnS     RATA TT.R" NORRAT. H

                FRR EMER, ANAERCTE-- EE, PN: ARE
                AGARYS , SteeAlayeTal A i.

                      "AHY N[el] A ii&'', literally "(two things) cannot be mentioned
                in the same breath", is used metaphorically to mean that there
                is no comparison between two people or two things because of By
                the significant difference. For instance, in terms of science and
                technology, there is no comparison between the past and the
                present.

Notes  The Ba-Sentence 1: AJEB + V +--+

       4, PETE AU AE A SA BSE            WANb a, ATLA "4" =F

       `J, mR "AFEB+ Vitec o eRFE" KaZ ILAa TRA . ar

       CSE. HE: AASITEN AA; BA SAW RAM, aEBk

       CAA. (lan:

               In Chinese, a ba-sentence can be used to indicate a certain action done on a definite
       person or thing, the structure being "AJB + V +--+ ". This type of ba-sentence is often
       used to request or order other people to do something. It is noteworthy that A is the agent
       of the action, and B is the patient. Both A and B should be specific or known to the speaker
       and the listener. For example:

                 (1) HIRIORA TY .

               (2) 4 RAAC i] K WIL
               (3) tr4etT& T ve,
               (4) AHS EHZAR

               ae wile IABSAE "FE" ASHI, BLA

                  If there is a negative adverb or a modal verb etc., it should precede "44". For example:

             (5) Riksta BK,

               (6) (RA 4OF LS
                (7) RAREFS BAAR
               (8) MTZV AK U T 4?

       EAL. PO:

               In Chinese, "A244" can be used after a numeral to indicate an approximate number,

       such as "= i 7-44" (around three o'clock), "t.#+A-4" (about five), and "4+-4E 7-4" (ten
       years or so). For example:

             (1) RR RAR G RR,

                In the saying "ttLZ", "sAt" refers to"a high-ranking
          official" or "a successful businessman". The saying means that one
          who holds a high government position or does big business often
          forgets certain trivialities due to the enormous amount of work.
          Now it is a polite way to say that it is understandable that one who
          is fully occupied with work or business affairs should forget some
          trivial matters, sometimes also conveying an ironic tone.

    Common _ Habit is a second nature

SAVING rage rok" | GH "AR" ARAL. BOAR REE, PM WRAY

                 TT, HRA AAAS, MERRIETT RR Be YJ
                 {ih, AES AES RBHRA, MRE, EE MASA A AY
                 , SINGRAAD RUMI, BAAD WEY «

                            In the saying "~J tis AK", "ne" means "to become".
                        The sentence means that when the way of doing something
                        turns into a habit, it becomes natural and hard to change.
                        It tells us to form good habits and not to think it's OK to
                        do something bad just once, because the bad deed may
                        gradually become a natural habit without you knowing it

                           and by then, it would be hard to discover or change it.

                    3. Zai da dianhua

                       qi chudng hou, yibidn chi zdofdn yibian kan bdozhi. Shi nian gudqu le, xidnzdi ta yijing
                       shi xidozhang le, yinwéi tai mang, méi tian zdoshang wo qi chudng hou dou kan bu dado ta,

          jt    $ #8 [4M Compound Complements of Direction

___Notes            DUH, "Shia ANe" MMB SAB, HORZNEMIIT IAL.

                         In Chinese, a compound complement of direction is formed by a verb and a simple
                 complement of direction following the verb, describing the direction of an action. For example:

                      (1) BFS R-ABAR,
                     (2) aA Te 3k HK
                       (3) RBEURF MHC,

                       (4) HAT RTUSARAKE--AIL,

                      FEALATATAI, BeWBCHE "OR" "A" AT. PAM:

                         When the object is a place, it should be put before "3/". For example:

                      (5) BIR RHR ER,
                     (6) Ho xe SUF RE,
                       (7) AKAM WAEEL,
                       (8) FRI, AF#NRLRKEE,

                      RSBE AT BE "He" "SE" aT, SURE "OR" "SE" Za.
                foi0l

                        When the object is a general item, it can be put either before or after "3/2". For

               (9) example: FFEI--NGHKA, --EERGR-AHA,

                      (10) M#AAS HRSUAR, =HKEFS RRZUA,

                        (11) SSA sp HFA, =ECEMH SH TMAR--H FLY,

                      (12) DMF QRRFS RH, --)MKVRRHF R,

                 FR: MRAKCATCMIFLM, PARR "OKRA" Ja. Leu:
                         SAPS EIR TTP, Ee EIR T --2ESLD.

                   Note: If the action has already been completed and realized, the object is usually put after
                            "/H". For example, "BF EFI [--~SPHIL. (My elder brother has bought
                            a watermelon.)" and "#7 [HI 46 [ --#£4L%]. (Dad has brought some gifts
                             home.)".

            7442 Pictophonetic Characters 2

                 LAWNS AA, Ae "KB RR" , hatebd i,
            FTE Pi. Pilon:

                   There are two types of pictophonetic characters which have a top-bottom structure.
            One has the semantic component on the top and the phonetic component at the bottom. For
            example:

                                                                                                                                        113
             FVEBF3

             Standard Course 3

              ee                RE              aa                  WH

             Character          _ Pronounciation -- Phonetic Component Semantic Component

                &               ba              & (ba)                                          R

                ss              ping            F (ping)                                        *

                  A--Aie "PRESS" , hate ere bi, Bae PW. pila:

                    The other has the phonetic component on the top and the semantic component at the
             bottom. For example:

              me                    BE                 ae             , jee

             Character          Pronounciation  Phonetic Component  Semantic Component

               48                  xiang          44 (xiang)                ses

                a                     ni            4a (nu)                  yy

Notes  The Ba-Sentence 3: A#EB + V + Complement of Result/Direction

            DLP, Zea RY RE HA BM SE AE SE BPR AE i EAE, BT
       FAS AFEB+V+25 24M A/S 4a" . PMN:

              In Chinese, the structure "AB + V + Complement of Result/Direction" can be used
       to indicate an action which is done on a definite person or thing and has brought a result to
       or has changed the position of the person or thing. For example:

             AfEB                        Vv  BRAN EE AN

          AFC FL                         i   FFT,

       4B YB IE RICA                     ie  TF,

       AACSz                             a   poeae

     Common.  First come, first served

     Saying       "FBR" SC a" BLAeR RCRA RUE ZR PAT BE, BSLAY
CRAB                FERS, SMR TREAT T BLE RFR Ln HE BU Ea, LE,

              RAAF.

                    In the saying "JE 4674" (first come, first served),                     7 hl
              "45" means "early". The saying means that due to the        LIMI | E Dd
              limited amount of something, the ones who come early        OF y E R
              will get it, while those who come late won't. Now it is
              usually used to encourage people to take the initiative in
              doing something and to be ready and get started as soon
              as possible.

      PBA BPH 4ZZz What Food Chinese People Eat on Their Birthdays
     FU AGE A ARE A RE, PRAM SRI. MRK KA, Hm NK
7e" WY. A, AE A Ae. PERT, DEE Bfe t Ae?

     Westerners eat cakes on their birthdays, while by tradition, Chinese people eat noodles.
The noodles, being long, stand for "longevity", which is why birthday noodles are also called
longevity noodles. What do people eat on their birthdays in your country?

Notes  The Structure "#02::---- (AiG), (S) ghweeeeeee"

       "WAR" Janae ---MBI, "RL" Jor ee ea BL

       PVE SR. TER, BoP               ee "mt" AYA. PMN:

              The clause following "dl52" is a hypothesis, and the clause after "#{" is the result
       based on the hypothesis. Note that the subject of the second clause should be put right
       before "st". For example:

       (1) HRIRART, RKAE---AIL,

       (2) WRIA, ABA,
       (3) WRA RHE, KRAHERF,

       (4) WRAL, RHAERRA--°FC,

                       wa "AS" Jaa --Beeoaini, nZd. pila:

                                 "4" is preceded by an adjective or a verb and followed by a verb phrase. For example:

                            (1) BFMMARIBLS AR,
                          (2) Foxe BRA T RM AK,
                              (3) LF RAFF T HEwLMa T .
                              (4) AME ARN ARAMRGA,

               AiR. Pld:

                       The form of a reduplicated disyllabic verb is "ABAB", such as "(A.A (K,.", indicating
               a short action or an attempt. For example:

                     (1) Mee HE? ARID H8 KATUGRUGR
                    (2) ea ATTe RAB BA F, E BY tere,
                     (3) RAAREB D F, D,
                      (4) RAMS BIBI .

            Ee lol¢tiels6A3 Flexible Use of Interrogative Pronouns 3

                    DUA HA, ATLA "iE" "{TA" "WEA" "RIL" eRe) (Re Sie A
               AMP IR, EARN eAS Yb., Bilan:

                      In Chinese, interrogative pronouns such as "iE", "{TA", "EA" and "WBJL" can be
               used to refer to every member in a certain scope, indicating all of them are the same in a
               certain way without exception. For example:

                    (1) Ha3gk)eAR

                     (2) AI Z ARS sk,

                    (3) MITEAZAABAYTM,

                       (4) seal Ae EATS LAB AS AFR

             é78=43 Pictophonetic Characters 3
                  Aa NIE a AB, BE "NBA" , tt etee ob ,

             PSE. PM:

                    There are two types of pictophonetic characters which have an inside-outside structure.
             One has the phonetic component inside the semantic component. For example:

                     aS            RE                 ae                   ize

                    Character  Pronounciation   Phonetic Component  Semantic Component

                       a           yuan            A (yuan)                  q
                       A           bing            ® (bing)                 x

             FFE "ABS" , aS eH, Fe TES DI. lM:

             The other has the semantic component inside the phonetic component. For example:

                     he             BRE               jaye                 eH

                    Character   Pronounciation  Phonetic Component  Semantic Component

                       ia            wen            ry (mén)                 a
                       i       ~ wén               1 (mén)

      iF    Oo  |              The Structure "2 .----- yee "

     Notes      "UB eee Bibs" MEB-- ANARNRSEEa, "USE" JAE ARE,

             "St" Jade. EA wee "RE" Ay, thal Wiikefe "RR" Ja.
            WRATH, BE "BR" WA. Bild:

                  "en . jit-+++-" is used to connect the two clauses of a conditional complex

            sentence, with "12" introducing a necessary condition and "i" followed by the
            consequent result. The subject can be put before or after "2 2". If there is a second subject
            in the sentence, it is put right before "jt". For example:

                  (1) RERA HT, HRRAI RH,

                   (2) M& RRSK, H-KAE,

                  (3) RRBL, KAI,

                (4) RBUARE, RAEN MC.

__Notes          DUES, Rm eMAiAEAVS E N Aa, WUT dea AE

                    In Chinese, a complement of direction can be used after a verb or an adjective to mean:
             different things.

                  shied "tk" , RANPM ERSA E. , PON:

                     "iH 5" can be used after a verb to indicate the emergence of something new. For
             example:

                    (1) AF ES R b RY?
                   (2) AAR RAAF READE,
                   (3) KGRAK, --DAILHBALAT,
                     4) BA TT LR IRABE KAT,

                  Sinn "HOR" HANA, GG AR, BO:

                     When used after a verb, "H{3" can also indicate the recognition of something
             implicit. For example:

                    (5) RPA TA? eM 2H?
                   (6) RAAT, LRAFAYMPALET.
                    (7) PBA Rix AR EA t+ ZA AF.
                    (8) RRA RA? BerAel e)y,

                   -- LETS ialakohialin "Re" 47m Hh BV FAGRENSS. FY TR BIeee
             th. falan:

                      When used after certain adjectives or verbs, " 3" indicates a change from movement
             to stillness, from being strong to being weak, or from being fast to being slow. For example:

                        ) AVIA R ARF ARIE, ETRY.
                    (10)KRFRS, BFNRBRT.
                   (11) RABAT AFR, RRL A
                    (12) RRMEEF PHY, eS LARUE AT.

                   Ain "EK" , AANA TAR. PMN:

                     When used after a verb, "2 3" indicates the act of recalling has reached a result. For
             example:

                    (13) tRizAC RAE HE T "A?
                   (14) 4 HE ARAL ARAB RA Z BAR OY 9
                   (15) AA, RABRREAFH T.
                   (16) RABACAT , WDM RRAT RT.

          The Chinese words "{#i", "|" and "jE" can all be followed by a pivotal element to
   mean "to cause". The three of them basically mean the same thing, with "{#*" usually used
   in written Chinese, while "l!\" usually used in spoken Chinese. For example:

         SVE AMA. "Be" FJRVISeAteY ATR "AY" "Gk" Jaa BETA
         fi) Mn :

                In Chinese, a sentence with "#%'' can express the passive voice, its structure being "S
         + #/NY/iE + O + V + Other Elements", in which the subject is the patient of the action and

         the object the agent. The object following "#%" can be left out, while the object after "4" or

         "jE" cannot. For example:

         is                 BMY /E  ia   ain                            Ath

         RK                 A       BMA  my                             y.

         Hey                AK      4    3,                             y.

         EH                 ik      pH   "EF,                           y.

         AB AA FL           aK      i    Pe                             T?

              FR, Areal MAR aS MABE "Be" AAT, Pld

                Note that negative adverbs and modal verbs should precede "#&" if there are any. For
         example:

                (1) PI AA RML R,
                (2) MAE RR FI KBB,
                (3) FAA WARIS ISKFIZAG,

               (4) RMB RRA ADA,

                 (2)RABIN, WT 7

                  BARA R, FHILAELA,

              HW 8 SR ES 1) Fd eS ARE, {EE 7FRE IES

                    In the saying "FIDLA A RR", "LY" means ~«
              "surely". The saying means that when a vehicle arrives
              at the foot of a mountain, it doesn't matter if its way is
              blocked by the mountain because there is always a way
              out. It is often used metaphorically to tell us not to fear
              the trouble or difficulty we face as there is always a
              solution to it.

   M4 CULTURE

                             PBA Z4limAfEIK Taboo Gifts in China

     SABE, SEAR LALM, HEAT ALBARN, ABER

    4 VEAL WIE ABIL , Eyeor RE PRRESE AAE R "BBC" eeeen
   7ST Fa "RE" 4G " AACE AHIAl Atal:mB. "Ke"
   lr] , SNe ae eaeBe. FEMME, BERL HE ZH en

          When visiting someone's home, we usually bring a gift. Remember not all gifts are
*, appropriate. In China, some articles cannot be given as gifts, such as umbrellas, shoes, and

 | clocks. The pronunciation of the Chinese character for "umbrella" is similar to that of the
    character for "separation", so giving someone an umbrella means to separate from the person.
    The character for "shoe" sounds the same as that for "evil", which bodes ill. The Chinese word
    for "giving a clock" sounds exactly the same as that for "attending upon a dying person". What
    taboos do you have'in your country regarding giving gifts?
